# A NEW CHEMICAL LIGHT
## CHEMICAL. <note>original: "CHYMICVM." In this era, "chemical" and "alchemical" were used interchangeably to describe the study of matter and its transformations.</note>

->*TO WHICH IS ADDED,*<-

# PREFACE.

A 2

## To all seekers of the chemical art <term>chemical art: chymice artis, a term for alchemy and the study of the properties of matter</term>, namely the genuine sons of Hermes <note>original: "Hermetis filiis." This refers to followers of Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy, implying the readers are dedicated students of the "Hermetic" tradition.</note>, the author prays for health and a blessing from God.

<image-desc>A large ornamental woodcut initial 'C' containing a winged figure with a human torso and foliate scrollwork forming its lower extremities.</image-desc>*When I was considering within myself, Honest Reader, how many counterfeit books and false recipes (as they call them) of the Alchemists—composed through the fraud and greed of imposters, and in which not even a spark of truth shines—are circulating among the seekers of natural and hidden arts; by which very many have been deceived, and are still being deceived: I decided I could do nothing more useful than to share the Talent <note>A reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents, suggesting that knowledge is a divine gift that must be invested and shared rather than hidden away.</note> entrusted to me by the Father of Lights <note>A scriptural title for God, emphasizing Him as the source of all wisdom and illumination.</note> with the sons and heirs of knowledge. So that posterity might also notice that not only in previous ages, but even in this age of ours, a singular*

<summary>In this preface, the author warns "the sons of Hermes" (alchemical students) about the prevalence of fraudulent texts and expresses a divinely inspired duty to share his genuine knowledge with future generations.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Hermes Trismegistus, imposters, divine wisdom, Father of Lights, chemical art, seekers of truth</keywords>

# PREFACE.

*...that philosophical blessing of God was not denied to certain men. For certain reasons, it did not seem advisable to publish my name, as I do not seek praise for myself, but rather strive to be useful to the lovers of Philosophy. Therefore, I leave the vain desire for glory to those who prefer to **SEEM** rather than to **BE** <note>original: "VIDERI quàm ESSE." A common philosophical maxim emphasizing that true character and substance are more important than public reputation.</note>. What I write here in testimony of the indubitable Philosophical truth—summarized in a few words—is drawn from manual experience <term>manual experience: manualis experientia, refers to practical laboratory work or "hand-work," emphasizing that his knowledge comes from doing, not just reading</term> granted to me by the Most High: so that those who have laid the first and real foundations in this most praiseworthy science might not, through this exhortation, abandon the practice of these most beautiful things; and thus be safe from the wicked crowd of smoke-sellers <term>smoke-sellers: Fumivendulorum, a derogatory term for alchemical charlatans who sold "smoke" or empty promises instead of real results</term>, to whom it is sweet to deceive. These are not dreams, as the ignorant common people say, nor are they the empty inventions of idle men, as the foolish and senseless—who laugh at the art—claim. It is the very Philosophical Truth; which, as a lover of truth, I neither should nor could hide or wrap in silence, as a support and confirmation of Chemical truth (which is often wrongly denounced). Although in these wicked times (where virtue and vice are treated as equals)*

<summary>The author explains his decision to remain anonymous to avoid vanity, asserting that his alchemical knowledge is based on successful laboratory experience rather than mere theory. He aims to protect serious students from fraudulent "smoke-sellers" who bring the art into disrepute.</summary>
<keywords>anonymity, manual experience, laboratory practice, Fumivenduli, philosophical truth, alchemy vs. fraud</keywords>

# PREFACE.

...on account of the unworthiness of the age and the ingratitude and faithlessness of men (to say nothing of the curses of the Philosophers), it [the Truth] greatly fears to come out into public. I could bring forward witnesses of this Chemical truth—namely, the most serious authors—according to the unanimous consensus of venerable Antiquity across so many different Nations. But those things which are seen by ocular experiment <term>ocular experiment: ocularis experientia, refers to direct observation or eye-witnessing of a laboratory result</term> do not require proof. Many men of both the highest and lowest status have seen that **DIANA** without her clothes <note>original: "sine veste DIANAM." An alchemical allegory referring to the discovery of the "naked" or pure secret of the Work, often associated with the purification of silver or the revelation of the Prima Materia.</note> in these past years (I speak as one who knows). 

And although certain idle troublemakers <note>original: "male feriati." Literally "those who keep holidays badly," meaning people who use their leisure for mischief or who are lazily misguided.</note> may be found, who—whether out of envy, malice, or fear of their impostures being detected—cry out that the soul can be extracted from gold and, through a pompous and vain delusion of display, be restored to another body; not without the loss and detriment of time, labor, and expense: let the sons of Hermes <term>sons of Hermes: Hermetis filij, a traditional name for sincere students and practitioners of alchemy</term> know for certain that the extraction of such "souls," as they call them—whether from Gold or from the Moon <note>original: "à Luna." In alchemical terminology, the Moon refers to Silver.</note> (by whatever common Alchemical way)—is a mere persuasion. This is indeed not believed by many, but at last through experience, the one and only mistress of truth...

->...our [endeavor], is proven with harm.<-

On the contrary, he who (by the **Philosophical way** <term>Philosophical way: via Philosophica, the true alchemical method based on the principles of the ancient sages rather than common chemistry</term>) can perform this without fraud or deceit—so that he tints even the smallest amount of metal, whether with profit or without profit, with the real color of the **Sun** <note>original: "Solis." In alchemical literature, the Sun is the standard symbol for Gold.</note> or the **Moon** <note>original: "Lunæ." The Moon represents Silver.</note> (remaining permanent through all required tests): I can rightly affirm that he has the doors of **Nature** open to investigate further and higher secrets, and to acquire them with the blessing of **GOD**.

Furthermore, I offer these present **Treatises**, written from my own experience, to the sons of knowledge: so that while they are engaged with every thought and movement of the mind in inquiring into the hidden operations of **Nature**, they may from there perceive and know the truth of things and **Nature** herself: in which thing alone the perfection of the entire most holy **Philosophical Art** consists, provided they proceed by the **Royal way** <term>Royal way: via Regia, the sovereign, direct, and natural path to the Philosopher's Stone</term> which **Nature** prescribes in all operations.

Therefore, I would here advise the **Honest reader** <note>original: "Candidum lectorem." A common greeting to a sincere and fair-minded student, literally the "bright" or "white" reader.</note> to understand my writings not so much from the "bark of the words" <note>original: "verborum cortice." A metaphor for the literal or superficial meaning of a text, suggesting the reader must look beneath the surface to find the true "pith" or secret.</note> as from the powers of **Nature**; lest he later deplore time, labor, and expense spent in vain. Let him be confident that this is the science of the **Wise** <note>original: "Sophorum." Referring to the Sages or Adepts of the Hermetic tradition.</note>, not of the unskilled: And that far different is the [language] of the Philosphers...

<meta>The catchword "rum" at the bottom indicates the next word is "Philosophorum" (of the Philosophers).</meta>

### PREFACE.

<sig>A 4</sig>

*...[the true] sense, rather than that it could be perceived by boastful braggarts* <note>original: "Thrasones gloriosi." Thraso was a stock character of a bragging soldier in Roman comedy; the author uses it here to describe arrogant pseudo-alchemists.</note>, *or mocking scholars, or those who are vicious against their own conscience (who, since they cannot rise by virtues, attempt it through crimes and slanders against honorable men), or by unskilled charlatans* <note>original: "circumforanei," literally "market-wanderers" or "quacks."</note> *who, with their **whitenings** and **reddenings*** <term>whitenings and reddenings: dealbationibus & rubefactionibus, superficial chemical processes that changed the surface color of a metal to look like silver or gold without changing its internal nature</term>—*bringing most unworthy disgrace to the most praised science of **Chemistry**—have deceived almost the entire world. For it is a gift of **God**; indeed, one can only arrive at it by the grace of **God** alone illuminating the intellect, through patient and religious humility (or through the visual demonstration of an experienced teacher). Therefore, **God** deservedly drives those who are estranged from Him far away from His secrets.*

*For the rest, I especially ask the **sons of the art** <term>sons of the art: filios artis, a traditional term for true initiates or dedicated students of alchemy</term> to accept with a grateful mind my zeal for serving them well: and when they have made the **HIDDEN MANIFEST** <note>original: "OCCVLTVM fecerint MANIFESTVM." A core alchemical principle: to make the internal, spiritual "gold" visible in the physical world.</note>, *and by **God's** will and constant labor have reached the desired **Philosophical harbor*** <note>original: "portum Philosophicum." The successful completion of the Great Work, often compared to a ship arriving safely at port after a long voyage.</note>, *let them, in the **Philosophical manner**, exclude all who are unworthy from this art: and mindful of love toward their needy neighbor in the fear of **God** (setting aside vain ostentation), to the **Best**—*

## WHAT IT TRULY IS,

<image-desc>A large ornamental woodcut initial 'M' depicting a winged mermaid-like figure with floral and scrollwork designs.</image-desc>
**MANY** wise and most learned men many centuries ago—indeed, according to Hermes <note>original: "Hermete teste." Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed; his "Emerald Tablet" was a cornerstone of alchemical thought.</note>, even before the flood—have written much concerning the preparation of the <term>Philosophers' Stone<gloss>The "Lapis Philosophorum," a legendary alchemical substance said to be capable of turning base metals into gold and acting as a universal medicine.</gloss></term>; and they have left so many writings for us that, unless **Nature** performed things believable to us every day, hardly anyone would still believe that **NATURE** was a reality. Because in former ages there were not so many inventors of things... <note>The Latin text ends mid-sentence with "fue-", which completes as "fuerunt" on the following page, meaning "there were."</note>

<summary>This page serves as the title and opening of the first treatise of "A New Chemical Light," establishing that alchemical knowledge dates back to antiquity and Hermes. The author argues that without the daily evidence provided by Nature itself, the ancient writings on the Philosophers' Stone would be dismissed as unbelievable.</summary>

*...to the Thrice-Greatest <note>original: "ter-Maximo." While "Optimo Maximo" (Best and Greatest) was the standard Roman address for Jupiter—and later the Christian God—alchemists frequently used "Thrice-Greatest" to align the Divine with the legendary founder of their craft, Hermes Trismegistus.</note> for this special gift, without misuse, within a silent and piously rejoicing heart, let them sing everlasting praises of gratitude.*

It reminded the reader that while the "false" alchemists used overly complex and expensive ingredients, the "true" secrets of Nature were believed to be elegant and simple.</note>

-uses it, or plainly ceases. In this same way, noble **Nature** always acts even unto the *Iliadum* <note>original: "Iliadum." For a certain permission was granted to **Nature** from the beginning, so that through her entire course she might arrive at better things and have full rest, toward which she strives with every effort; she rejoices in her own end, no differently than an ant in its old age, for whom **Nature** creates wings in its final years <note>A traditional folk belief and alchemical metaphor suggesting that once a creature reaches its final peak or "wings," its natural cycle is concluding.</note>. For the *Art of Chemistry* has now found such subtleties that scarcely greater ones can be discovered; and it differs from the **Art** of the ancient **Philosophers** as much as a **Watchmaker** <note>original: "Faber Horologiorum."</note> differs from a simple **Blacksmith** <note>original: "Faber Ferrarius."</note>. ->### CHEMICAL TREATISE<-

in that in which it begins. I have stated that there is only one **Nature** <note>original: "Natura." In this context, Nature is viewed as a semi-divine administrative force acting on behalf of God.</note>, through which **God** performs all things. It is not that **God** would be unable to work without her—for He Himself created **Nature** and is all-powerful—but it simply pleased Him to do it this way. All things come forth from that one **Nature**; nor is there anything in the world outside of **Nature**. 

And although sometimes "abortions" <note>original: "abortus." In alchemical philosophy, this refers to failed results, malformed minerals, or "monstrosities" in the natural world that do not reach their intended perfection.</note> occur, this is not the fault of **Nature**, but rather the fault of either the craftsman or the location. This **Nature** is divided into four places <note>Likely referring to the four Aristotelian elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth.</note>, in which she performs all these things that appear to us and those that are in shadow; for indeed, things exist in shadow rather than truly appearing as they are. 

She changes into male and female, and is compared to **Mercury** <term>Mercury: In alchemy, this is the principle of "volatility" and "transmutation" that mediates between the solid and the spiritual.</term> because she separates into various places. According to the different regions of the earth, she produces things both good and bad, even though there are no truly "bad" places on **Earth**, at least as it appears to us. There are, however, only four **Qualities** <note>Heat, Cold, Moisture, and Dryness.</note>, and these are present in all things. They never exist in perfect harmony; one always exceeds the others. **Nature**, however, is not vi-

->### NEW LIGHT<-

[invi]sible <note>The word completes "invisibilis" (invisible) from the end of the previous page.</note>, although she acts visibly; for she is a **volatile spirit** <term>Volatile spirit: A subtle, active force that moves within physical matter, often associated with the alchemical principle of Mercury.</term> who performs her duty within bodies: she has her place and seat in the **Divine Will**. For us, in this place, she is of no use except that we might know her locations, which are more suitable or closer to her; that is, <margin>/</margin> so that we might know how to join thing to thing according to **Nature** <note>original: "Natura."</note>, so that wood is not mixed with man, nor an ox or animal with metal; <margin>//</margin> but let each thing act upon its own kind: and then **Nature** will also perform her duty. The place of **Nature** is none other than—as stated above—in the will of **God**.

## NOVUM LUMEN

The four **Elements** project their qualities. Just as a man emits his seed into the **womb** <term>Matrix: The womb or generative environment where nature forms new substances.</term> of a woman, in which nothing of the seed remains, but after the womb has received its due portion, it casts the rest out; similarly, it happens in the center of the earth that the **Magnetic force** <note>original: "vis Magnetica"; refers to a natural power of attraction that draws specific materials together to initiate growth.</note> of a certain location attracts to itself a substance suitable for giving birth to something; the rest is cast out into stones and other **excrements** <note>original: "excrementa"; in alchemy, this refers to the dross, slag, or waste products left over after a substance has been refined or "born."</note>. 

For all things have their origin from that source, nor is anything in the whole world born except through this source. 

For example: imagine a small vessel of water on a flat table, placed in the middle of the table, and around it let various things and various colors be placed, likewise salt, and so on, each one separately. If the water is then poured into the middle, you will see that water flow here and there; and when one streamlet touches a red color, it will be turned red by it; if it reaches the salt, it will take on the flavor of salt, and so on for the rest. For the water does not change the locations, but the diversity of the locations changes the water.

<meta>catchword: Simili (Similarly)</meta>

<summary>This page explains how the central "source" or seed of nature is distributed throughout the earth, taking on the specific qualities (like color or saltiness) of the locations it encounters. It uses the analogy of water flowing across a table to illustrate how a single substance becomes many diverse things.</summary>

The seed and sperm of all things is unique and one, and yet it generates various things, as is shown by the following example.

A man’s seed is a noble seed, and was created at least for the **generation of man**; nevertheless, if a man abuses it (which is within his free will), an **abortion** <note>original: "abortus"; in this alchemical context, it refers to a "monstrous" or failed birth—a substance that has not reached its intended perfection or has been corrupted by an unsuitable environment.</note> is born. Through such inhumane and detestable **mixing** of men with beasts, various beasts would be produced that resemble men. For so it happens: if the sperm enters the center, that which ought to be born from there is born; but when it has already [come] into a place...

# THIRD TREATISE.

## *On the true first matter of Metals.*

The first matter of Metals is twofold, but one without the other does not create metal. <margin>//</margin> The first and principal matter is the **Moisture** mixed with the heat of the air; this <margin>//</margin> the **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"; in this context, it specifically refers to "Adepts" or Alchemists who have mastered the secrets of nature.</note> have named **Mercury**, which <margin>//</margin> is governed by the rays of the sun and moon in the **Philosophical sea** <note>original: "mari Philosophico"; a metaphorical term for the liquid substance or solvent used in the alchemical process.</note>: The second is the **dry heat** of the earth <margin>//</margin> which they have called **sulfur**. # FOURTH TREATISE.

->*How metals are generated in the bowels of the earth.*<-

Metals are produced in this manner: after the four Elements have cast their virtues into the center of the earth, the **Archeus** <term>Archeus: in alchemy and Paracelsian medicine, the internal "master-alchemist" or vital force that governs the growth and transformation of all things in nature.</term>, by distilling, sublimates them by the heat of perpetual motion toward the surface of the earth. For the earth is porous, and the **wind** <note>original: "ventus"; here referring to a subtle, spiritual vapor rather than moving air</note> is resolved through the pores of the earth by trickling into water, from which all things are born. Therefore, let the **sons of doctrine** <note>a traditional term for students of alchemy or those initiated into its secrets</note> know that the **seed of metals** is no different from the seed of all things—namely, a **moist vapor**. Because of this, **Artists** <note>referring to practitioners of the alchemical "Art"</note> seek in vain the reduction of metals into their **first matter** <term>materia prima: the original, undifferentiated substance from which all physical things are formed, often considered impossible to isolate in its pure state.</term>, which is nothing but vapor. The **Philosophers** <note>referring to the authoritative writers of the alchemical tradition</note> did not understand the "first matter" in such a way, but rather as the **second matter**, as **Bernard of Treviso** <note>original: "Bernardus Treuisanus"; a 15th-century alchemist famous for his long search for the Stone and his writings on the "natural" path of alchemy</note> argues most excellently, though not quite clearly, because regarding the four Elemen-

<meta>The text cuts off mid-word ("Elementis") as indicated by the catchword "sti" at the bottom of the original page, which would begin the next page's first word.</meta>

<summary>This page explains the subterranean generation of metals, attributing their creation to the "Archeus" which distills elemental virtues into a moist vapor or "seed." It clarifies that the true first matter of metals is a vapor, and that alchemists should focus on "second matter" as described by Bernard of Treviso.</summary>

<keywords>Archeus, generation of metals, first matter, second matter, Bernard of Treviso, elemental virtues, moist vapor, alchemical theory</keywords>

# CHEMICAL [TREATISE]

...speaks of [the truth]; <note>The text continues from the previous page, likely completing the phrase "speaks of the truth."</note> nevertheless, he wished to say this, and spoke only to the **sons [of the art]** <note>A traditional term for students of alchemy or those initiated into its secrets.</note>. But I, in order to more clearly reveal the **Theory** <term>Theorica: the underlying principles or conceptual framework of the alchemical art, as opposed to the manual labor of the laboratory.</term>, wished to warn everyone here to cease so many **solutions**, so many **circulations**, so many **calcinations**, and **reiterations** <note>These refer to common alchemical laboratory processes: dissolving a solid (solution), distilling in a continuous loop (circulation), heating a substance to a powder (calcination), and repeating these steps (reiteration).</note>; for that which is sought in a hard thing is sought in vain, since by its nature it is everywhere soft. 

Let not the **first matter** be sought, but only the **second matter**, such a kind indeed which, as soon as it is conceived, cannot be changed into another form. But if you ask how a **metal** <note>original: "metallum"</note> may be reduced into such a matter, I follow the intention of the **Philosophers** <note>Referring to the authoritative authors of the alchemical tradition, such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</note> in this: I only desire this above all else, that the sons of the art understand the meaning of the writers and not just their syllables, and where **Nature** <note>original: "Natura"</note> finishes—namely, in the metallic bodies perfected before our eyes—there the **Art** must begin. 

But to return to the point (since our intention here is not only about the **Stone** <note>original: "lapide"; referring to the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold.</note>), let us now discuss the **matter of metals**. A little before, I said all things are born from liquid air or vapor, which the **Elements** <term>Elements: the four classical building blocks of the universe—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</term> by perpetual

...by motion, they drip into the bowels of the earth. There are those who think that **Lead** <note>original: "Saturnum"</note> has one seed, **Gold** <note>original: "aurum"</note> another, and so on for the remaining metals; but these ideas are vain. There is only a single seed; the same is found in Lead as is found in Gold, and the same in **Silver** <note>original: "Luna"</note> as in **Iron** <note>original: "Marte"</note>. However, the location in the earth was different, if you understand me correctly; although in Silver, **Nature** <note>original: "Natura"</note> ceased her work sooner than in **Gold** <note>original: "Sole," literally "the Sun"</note>, and likewise for the others. 

For when that vapor is sublimated from the center of the earth, it passes through places that are either cold or hot. If, therefore, the vapor passes through hot and pure places, where the **fatness of sulfur** <note>original: "pinguedo sulphuris"</note> clings to the walls, that vapor—which the **Philosophers** <note>The established authorities of the alchemical tradition.</note> called the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercury of the Philosophers: not common quicksilver, but a refined, essential "water" or vapor that acts as the primary material for the Great Work.</term>—adapts itself and joins with that fatness, which it afterwards sublimes with itself; and then it becomes an **oiliness** <note>original: "vnctuositas," referring to a thick, oily substance</note>, leaving behind the name of vapor and ac- <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing as "accipit" (receives) or "acquirit" (acquires) on the next page.</note>

<margin><image-desc>A manicula (pointing hand) in the right margin points towards the beginning of the fifth line of text.</image-desc></margin> For the more a location is purified, the more beautiful the metals it returns; for it must be known that this vapor continually goes out from the center to the surface, and in its going, it cleanses the locations. And this is the **reiteration** and **circulation of Nature** <note>original: "circulatio Naturæ"</note>: it is sublimated for so long, producing new things, until the location is optimally purified; and the more it is purified, the more noble the things it produces. **Metals**, however, are created in this way: the earth is purified by a long **distillation** <term>Distillation: here, the natural geological process where internal heat causes vapors to rise through the earth's crust, refining the soil as they pass.</term>, and afterwards they are generated by the addition of **fatness** <note>original: "pinguedinis"; the oily or sulfurous principle of alchemy that provides the "body" of a metal.</note>. Just as experience itself testifies, that **gold** is sought and found nowhere else but in the mountains, which happens in the plains only rarely—it cannot be; for most often such places are damp, not with vapor but with **Elemental water**, which draws that vapor to itself, and they embrace each other so that they are not easily separated; afterwards the **Sun of heaven** <note>original: "Sol cœli"</note> digesting...

Let him therefore bring it about that the **spirit is separated from the water**, so that it may be purified, and the **grain** <note>The "grain" refers to the crystalline seed or the "philosopher's stone" in its early form.</note> may appear. Afterward, having cast aside the **dregs** <note>original: "faecibus" — the waste or sediment left after distillation.</note>, let him lead the spirit back from on high into the water, and cause them to join together; for that union will generate a **branch** <note>An alchemical metaphor for the growth of a new, perfected substance.</note> unlike its parents in form.

# SIXTH TREATISE.

->*Concerning the Second Matter, and the putrefaction*<-
->*of things.*<-

We have spoken of the **first matter** <term>Prima materia: the fundamental, formless substance from which all natural things originate.</term> of things, and how things are born from **Nature** without seed—that is, how Nature receives from the **Elements** the matter from which it generates seed. Now, however, we shall begin a discourse concerning **seed**, and things generated from seed. Every thing that possesses seed is multiplied within it, but this does not happen without the help of Nature. For seed is nothing other than **congealed air** <note>original: "aer congelatus" — In alchemical theory, air was considered the carrier of the vital life-spirit; "congealed" refers to it taking a fixed, physical form.</note> within some body, or a **moist vapor**; and unless this is dissolved by a warm vapor as well, it is of no use. 

Therefore, let the investigators of the **art** <note>The "art" refers to Alchemy, specifically the practical work of transformation.</note> know what seed is, lest they seek a thing which does not exist; and let them know that it is **threefold**, having been produced from the four elements. The first is **MINERAL**, of which we speak in this place; the second is **VEGETABLE**... <note>The text breaks off here at the start of the word "Vegetable."</note>

...**VEGETABLE**; the third is **ANIMAL**. The **MINERAL** seed is known by the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophers: In this context, the term refers to "Natural Philosophers" or Alchemists who study the hidden laws of nature.</term>; the Vegetable is common and ordinary, as we see in fruits; the Animal is known through imagination <note>original: "ex imaginatione" — In alchemical theory, the "imagination" was considered a literal formative force that could shape physical matter, particularly during the generation of life.</note>. The Vegetable seed shows us how nature creates it from the four **Elements**.

For it must be known that winter is the cause of **putrefaction** <term>Putrefaction: A state of decay or breakdown that alchemists believed was the necessary first step for any new growth or transformation.</term>, since it congeals the **vital spirits** <note>original: "spiritus vitales" — The subtle, invisible substances responsible for life and movement within a body.</note> in the trees; and when these are dissolved by the heat of the sun (in which there is a **magnetic force** <note>original: "vis magnetica" — This reflects the early scientific belief that the sun acted as a giant magnet, drawing moisture and "spirits" upward from the earth.</note> attractive of all moisture), then the **heat of nature**, excited by movement, drives a subtle vapor of water to the circumference, which opens the pores of the tree and causes drops to drip, always separating the pure from the impure.

However, the pure sometimes precedes the impure; the pure remains and is congealed into flowers, the impure goes into the leaves, and the gross and thick into the bark; the bark of the tree remains fixed, the leaves fall due to cold or heat when its pores are obstructed; the flowers are congealed in such a color as the heat itself possesses, and

# CHEMICAL TREATISE
### 35

...in the **bowels of the earth** <note>original: "in terræ viſceribus"</note>. What special privilege do **plants** <note>original: "vegetabilia" — referring to the entire vegetable kingdom.</note> possess over **metals** that God would bestow a seed upon them while unfairly excluding the latter? Are metals not of the same worth in the eyes of God as trees? Let this be established as a certainty: **nothing grows without a seed**; for where there is no seed, the thing is dead.

It is therefore necessary that either the **four elements** <term>Four Elements: The traditional alchemical building blocks of matter—Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</term> create the **seed of metals**, or that they produce metals without a seed. If they are produced without a seed, they cannot reach perfection; for every thing lacking a seed is imperfect in its very composition. Anyone who does not grant belief to this unquestionable truth is unworthy to investigate the secrets of nature; for nothing is born in this world that is devoid of a seed. 

The **seed of metals** is truly and actually implanted within them, and its generation occurs in this way: In the first operation of nature, the **four elements** distill a **heavy watery vapor** into the center of the earth by means of the **Archeus of Nature** <term>Archeus: The "master chemist" or vital force within nature that directs the formation of substances.</term>. This vapor is the **seed of metals**, and it is called **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius" — In alchemy, this refers not to common quicksilver, but to the "Philosophical Mercury," the fundamental watery principle or "sperm" of all metals.</note>.

### 38

That thing whose form is contained in the seed will be easily generated through the operation of Nature. Here, the **Artificer** <term>Artificer: original: "Artifex" — The alchemical practitioner or chemist who assists and directs natural processes rather than forcing them.</term> does nothing except know how to **separate the subtle from the dense** <note>original: "subtile à spisso separare" — A fundamental alchemical operation involving the purification of matter by removing "gross" or impure parts.</note> and place it in the required vessel. For this must be considered: just as a thing is begun, so it is finished. From one come two; from two, one—and no further. 

<margin># milije. <note>This marginal note is likely a shorthand reference or a corruption of a Latin term; its precise meaning in this context is unclear.</note></margin>

There is one God; from this one God, the Son was begotten. One gave two, and two gave one—the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritum Sanctum"</note> proceeding from both. Thus was the world made, and thus shall be its end. Consider these previous four points most exactly: you have in them the Father, the Father and the Son, and finally the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: "SS" — Abbreviation for *Spiritum Sanctum*.</note>. You have the four **Elements**; you have four **luminaries** <note>original: "luminaria" — Sources of light or influence; here, the author distinguishes between the visible stars/sun and the hidden, "central" fires of the earth.</note>, two Celestial and two Central. Nothing more exists, ever has, or ever will be than what appears in this figure <note>The author refers to a symbolic diagram or a conceptual "figure" representing the relationship between the Trinity, the elements, and the creation of matter.</note>. If I were to record all the mysteries to be drawn from this, a massive volume would arise. 

I return to the purpose and tell you the truth, my son: one is not created from one, because this is the property of God alone. Let it be enough for you that you are able to create from two one thing that is useful to you. Know, therefore, that the **seed**...

# SEVENTH TREATISE
## *On the power of the second matter.*

But so that you may more easily understand what kind of thing this second matter is, I shall describe for you the powers by which you may recognize it. First, you should know that Nature is divided into three **Kingdoms** <note>original: "Regna"</note>. There are two Kingdoms, either of which can stand on its own, even if the other two did not exist; these are the **Mineral**, **Vegetable**, and **Animal** kingdoms. The Mineral kingdom can subsist by itself, even if there were no humans in the world, nor any tree or herb. Likewise, the Vegetable kingdom could exist, even if there were no metal and no animal...

Experience also testifies that men who indulge too much in **Venus** <note>original: "veneri" — a common historical euphemism for sexual activity, named after the Roman goddess of love.</note> become weaker, just as trees bearing excessive fruit later become sterile.

# EIGHTH TREATISE.

## *How nature works in the Seed by means of art.*

Every seed is of no importance on its own if it is not placed, either by **art** <note>original: "arte" — here referring to the alchemical art or human intervention.</note> or by **Nature**, into its proper **womb** <note>original: "matricem" — in alchemical thought, the "matrix" is any vessel or environment, like soil or a uterus, that receives a seed to nurture it.</note>. And although the seed itself is more noble than any other creature, it is the womb that provides its life; it is what causes the sperm or the grain to **putrefy** <term>Putrefy: original: "putrescere" — In alchemy, putrefaction is the essential first stage of transmutation, where a substance breaks down so that a new, more perfected form can emerge.</term> and brings about the **congealing** <note>original: "congelationem"</note> of that pure point of essence. Furthermore, by the heat of its own body, the womb nourishes the seed and causes it to grow. 

This process occurs in all three of the previously mentioned kingdoms of nature—the animal, vegetable, and mineral—and it happens naturally over the course of months, years, and seasons. Truly, that art is wise which is able to shorten the time of growth in the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, though it cannot do so in the animal kingdom. In the mineral kingdom specifically, art perfects that which nature is unable to finish because of the **crude air** <note>original: "aerem crudum" — often referring to external environmental factors or "unrefined" spiritual influences that hinder the ripening of metals in the earth.</note>, which by its harsh force has filled the pores of the body

->*on the drawing out of the Metallic*<-

We have spoken of **Nature**, of **Art**, of the **body**, and of **sperm** and **seed**; now let us descend to practice: namely, how metals ought to be mixed together, and what kind of correspondence exists between them. Know, therefore, that the woman does not differ from the man; both are born from the same seed and in a single **womb** <note>original: "matrice"</note>. Nothing was lacking except **digestion** <term>Digestion: In alchemy, this refers to a slow, steady heat (often compared to the warmth of the belly) that matures, "cooks," or ripens a substance over time.</term>. And just as a womb that is purer in blood and **salt** <note>original: "sale"</note> [produces a healthy child], so **Silver** <note>original: "Luna" (the Moon) — the alchemical name for Silver.</note> comes from the same seed as **Gold** <note>original: "Sole" (the Sun) — the alchemical name for Gold.</note>, and from the same womb. However, the womb [of Silver] contained more water than **digested blood** <note>original: "sanguine digesto"</note>, according to the timing of the celestial Moon. Truly, so that you may more easily imagine how metals **unite** <note>original: "coeant" — to come together or copulate; used here to describe chemical or natural bonding.</note> so that they may emit and receive seed: look upon the heavens and the [spheres] of the **planets** <note>original: "planetarum"</note>...

# CHEMICAL [MATTERS]

# THE TENTH TREATISE.

## *On the Supernatural Generation*
## *of the Son of the Sun:* <note>original: "filij Solis" — Alchemically, the "Son of the Sun" refers to the Philosophers' Stone or the perfected, "living" gold produced by the work.</note>

We have treated those things which Nature creates, and which God has created; so that the searchers of the art <note>original: "scrutatores artis" — Those practicing the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> might more easily understand the possibility of nature. The **Philosophers' Stone** or **tincture** <term>Tincture: A potent alchemical substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold or silver, or acting as a medicine to "color" or perfect the substance it touches.</term> is nothing else than **Gold** <note>original: "Aurum"</note> **digested** <term>Digestion: A process of constant, gentle heat used to mature or "ripen" a substance over time, mimicking the warmth of the earth or the womb.</term> to the highest degree: for common **Gold** is like a herb without seed; when it matures, it produces seed; so gold, when it matures, gives forth seed or a tincture. ### CHYMICUM. 57

**central heat** <term>central heat</term> <note>original: "calor centralis"</note>, which is perpetual, acts upon it, it causes the water to distill and become heated. Thus, by the power of the heat, that water is turned into **air** <note>original: "aerem" — in this context, the author refers to vapor or gaseous state</note>, and in this way it bursts forth to the surface of the earth, for it cannot endure being enclosed. Once it has cooled there, it is resolved back into water. Meanwhile, in opposite places, it also happens that not only air, but even water escapes; this is seen when dark clouds are violently raised into the air. 

I offer you this household example of the matter: if you boil water in a pot, you will see gentle vapors and winds produced by a slow fire, but with a more intense fire, thicker clouds will appear. The **central heat** <term>central heat</term> works in exactly the same way: it lifts the subtle water into the air, and that which is thick—derived from **salt** <term>salt</term> <note>original: "sale" — the alchemical principle of physical form and solidity</note> or **fatness** <term>fatness</term> <note>original: "pinguedine" — often referring to the alchemical principle of Sulfur, an oily, fertile substance</note>—it distributes to the earth, from which various things are born. The remaining residue becomes rocks and stones. 

Someone might object that if this were the case, then it ought to happen continuously, yet we often feel no wind. I reply that if [water] is not poured violently into a **distilling vessel** <term>distilling vessel</term> <note>original: "vas distillatorium" — an alchemical flask or alembic used for heating and separating substances</note>...

### [Page] 58

...through which no wind is produced, for a small amount of water excites only a small wind. You see that **thunder** <note>original: "tonitrua"</note> is not always produced, even if there is rain and wind, but only when turbid water is carried by the force of the air toward the **sphere of fire** <term>sphere of fire</term> <note>In the traditional Aristotelian cosmology used by alchemists, the "sphere of fire" was the highest region of the atmosphere, located just below the moon, where the element of fire resided.</note>; for fire cannot endure water. You have an example of this before your eyes when you pour cold water into a hot **furnace** <note>original: "fornacem"</note>, from which "thunders" <note>The author is comparing the crackling and popping of steam in a furnace to the sound of thunder in the sky.</note> are provoked. 

But as to why water does not enter those places and cavities uniformly, the reason is that there are many such vessels and locations. Sometimes, one cavity uses winds to drive the water away from itself for several days or months, until a **striking back** <note>original: "repercussio" — a return or recoil</note> of the water occurs. We see this in the sea, whose waves are sometimes driven for many miles before they meet a resistance that causes them to return. 

But let us return to our subject. I say that fire or heat is the cause of the **movement of the air** <note>original: "motus aeris"</note> and the life of all things; and the earth is the **nurse** <term>nurse</term> <note>original: "nutrix" — a frequent alchemical term for the element or environment that sustains and "feeds" a substance as it develops.</note> or **receptacle** <note>original: "receptaculum"</note> of all these. But if there were no water to cool the earth and our air, then the ear[th]—

# EPILOGUE OR
## Conclusion of These
### *Twelve Treatises.*

I have written, friend reader, the preceding twelve Treatises for the sake of the **sons of art** <term>filiorum artis</term> <note>A traditional term for alchemists or initiates into the Hermetic mysteries.</note>, so that before they apply their hands to the work, they might have knowledge of **nature's operation** <term>naturae operationem</term>, namely, how she produces things through her working. I have done this lest they enter the gate without keys, and draw water with a sieve; for he labors in vain who stretches out his hands to the tasks without a knowledge of nature. In this holy and most true **science** <term>scientia</term>, he who has no sun shining for him dwells in the darkness of night; he for whom the moon does not appear by night exists in a thick shadow. Nature has its own light, which is not accessible to our eyes; the shadow of nature

is a body to our eyes: but if the **light of nature** <term>lumen naturae</term> <note>In Hermetic philosophy, the "light of nature" is an intuitive or divinely granted insight that allows the alchemist to understand the hidden workings of the universe that are invisible to the physical eye.</note> illuminates anyone, the mist is immediately lifted from their eyes, and without hindrance they can perceive the point of **our magnet** <term>magnetis nostri</term> <note>Sendivogius often refers to a "magnet" as a substance or attractive force capable of drawing the "vital air" or "celestial saltpeter" down to earth.</note> which corresponds to both centers of the rays—namely, those of the sun and the earth. I have revealed here; pray to God that He may prosper your undertakings: And so in this place you will have the truest explanation of **Hermes** <note>original: "Hermetis" — referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> asserting that its **Father is the sun, its mother indeed the moon, and that the wind carried it in its belly** <note>original: "Patrem eius esse solem, matrem verò lunam, & quod ventus portauerit illum in ventre suo" — a direct quote from the "Emerald Tablet," one of the most famous and foundational texts of Western alchemy.</note>; namely the **Alkali salt** <term>sal Alkali</term>, which the Philosophers have named **Sal Armoniac** <term>Sal Armoniacum</term> and **vegetable [salt]**, hidden in the belly of **magnesia** <note>In alchemy, "magnesia" rarely refers to the modern element magnesium; it usually signifies a "primordial earth" or a mineral substance that acts as a "mother" or container for the secret spirit.</note>.

Farewell, benevolent reader, and enjoy these labors—verified through my long experience—for the glory of the Divine name, the salvation of your soul, and the benefit of your neighbor.

# TO THE SONS OF TRUTH

## Preface to the Riddle
### *Philosophical.*

Now to you, **sons of science** <note>Initiates or students of alchemy who seek the "true" knowledge of nature rather than mere wealth.</note>, I have revealed everything from the source of the universal fountain, so that nothing more remains. For in my preceding **Treatises** <note>Referring to the twelve treatises that comprise Michael Sendivogius’s *New Light of Alchemy*.</note> I have sufficiently explained nature through examples. I have manifested both **theory and practice** <note>In alchemy, "theory" refers to the underlying natural philosophy, while "practice" (praxis) refers to the laboratory work.</note> as far as was permitted. But lest anyone should complain of **conciseness** <term>laconism</term> <note>original: "Laconismo." A term for extreme brevity of speech or writing, named after the Spartans of Laconia. The author is concerned he may have been too brief for some readers to follow.</note>, as if I had omitted something, I will still describe the whole art to you enigmatically; so that you may see how far I have come by the guidance of God. There are infinite books treating this art; yet in hardly any will you find the truth so far revealed to you. I wished to provide this for the following reason: because I have spoken with many who believed they understood the writings of the **Philosophers** <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to the "Adepts" or master alchemists of the past.</note> well; but I discovered they

<meta>catchword: ea-</meta>

<summary>In this preface, the author addresses his readers as "Sons of Truth," claiming to have already revealed the secrets of nature and the laboratory but offering a final "riddle" to ensure the full art is understood. He justifies this addition by noting that many people mistakenly believe they understand the cryptic writings of the ancient masters when they actually do not.</summary>

<keywords>Sons of Truth, alchemy, philosophical riddle, laconism, theory and practice, Michael Sendivogius, alchemical treatises</keywords>

explained those same writings far more subtly than nature—which is simple—required. Indeed, all my truthful sayings seemed too common and unbelievable to those who always consider themselves highly wise. It happened several times that I suggested the art to certain people word for word, but they were by no means able to grasp it; they did not believe there was **water in our sea** <note>original: "aquam in mari nostro." A famous alchemical metaphor. The "sea" often represents the universal solvent or the "Mercury of the Philosophers," which appears common but contains the secret of the Work.</note>, and yet they still wished to be seen as **Philosophers** <term>Adepts</term>. Since, therefore, they could not understand my words even when spoken by mouth, I do not fear (as other Philosophers have feared) that anyone might grasp the secret too easily; I say it is a **gift of God** <note>original: "Donum Dei." A recurring theme in alchemy: that no matter how much one studies, the final realization only comes through divine grace or "illumination."</note>.

It is true indeed that if the study of **Chemistry** <note>original: "Chymico." In the 17th century, "Chymistry" encompassed both what we now call chemistry and alchemy without distinction.</note> required the sharp insight of a subtle mind, and if the matter were of such a kind that it could be seen by the eyes of the common people, I have seen their minds to be fit enough to investigate such things. But I tell you: be simple, and do not be too clever, until you have found the **secret** <term>arcanum</term>, for once you possess it, wisdom will necessarily burn within you. Then you will not lack the ease of writing infinite books, which will be far easier for him who is in the **center** <meta>catchword: tro (centro)</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the "wise" fail to understand alchemy because they overlook its simplicity, whereas the true art is a gift from God that requires a simple mind rather than over-intellectualization.</summary>

He learned how to treat **Mercury**, and afterwards how to **sublime** <note>A process where a solid substance is heated until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used to purify chemicals.</note> it and **calcine** <note>Heating a substance to high temperatures in air to change its physical or chemical structure, often reducing it to a powder or "calx."</note> it in wondrous ways: now with salts, now with sulfur, now with metals, now with minerals, now with blood, now with hair, now with **strong waters** <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>Nitric acid or other corrosive mineral acids used by early chemists to dissolve metals.</note>, now with herbs, urine, and vinegar. But he could find nothing to suit his purpose; he left nothing in the world untouched with which he might treat his "good" Mercury.

And when he was unable to make any progress at all, he came across this saying: *that it is found in the dunghill* <note>original: "in sterquilinio invenitur." A famous alchemical paradox suggesting that the most precious substance—the Stone—has its origins in the most common, base, or "vile" matter.</note>. He began to treat Mercury with various dungs, both together and separately. And when he had become so exhausted and full of anxious thoughts, he fell asleep. 

And in his dreams a vision appeared to him: an **Old Man** came to him, greeted him, and said: "Friend, why are you so sad?" He replied, "I would gladly make the **Philosophers' Stone**." Then the other said, "Friend, from what materials do you wish to make the Philosophers' Stone?" 

***Alchemist.*** From Mercury, Lord. 

***Old Man.*** From what kind of Mercury, then? 

***Alchemist.*** There is but one Mer-

*Alch.* I myself am a **Philosopher** <term>Philosophus: Here, a "natural philosopher" or true alchemist who claims to understand the secret laws of nature, as opposed to a mere "puffer" or fraudulent gold-maker.</term>.

*Alch.* Oh, what do I hear? Surely this is the **Devil**: for I do everything according to the writings of the **Philosophers**, and I know best how to work.

*Merc.* You know "best" indeed: for you do even more than you know and read. For the **Philosophers** said that nature must be mixed with natures <note>original: "naturam naturis miscendam"</note>, and they command nothing outside of nature; but you now mix me with almost every kind of filthy thing, even with dung.

...I become a shadow. *Alch.* Tell me then, who are you in your center? And I will torture you no more. *Merc.* Now I am forced to speak with you from the very foundation. ### CHEMICAL.

Know this: every traveler always strives toward his own homeland, and when he arrives at the place from which he departed, he rests; and he always returns wiser than when he left. <note>This is an alchemical metaphor for "circulation"—the process of distilling a substance so it rises as vapor and returns to a liquid state, supposedly becoming more pure or "wise" each time.</note>

*Alch.* Will you return again sometime?

*Merc.* I return, but in another form.

*Alch.* I do not understand what this means, nor do I understand anything about the fire.

*Merc.* If anyone knows the fire of my heart, he has seen that fire <gloss>the due heat</gloss> <note>original: "calorem debitum." In alchemy, this refers to the precise, moderate temperature—often compared to the heat of a brooding hen—required for the "Great Work" to succeed.</note> is my food; and the longer the spirit of my heart consumes the fire, the fatter <note>In this context, "fatness" refers to the substance becoming more enriched with "sulfur" or "tincture," increasing its medicinal or transformative power.</note> it will be. Its death is afterwards the life of all things that are in this kingdom where I am.

*Alch.* Are you great?

*Merc.* Take me as an example: out of a thousand little drops I will become one, and from one I give many thousands of drops. And just as my body is before your eyes, if you know how to play with me, you can divide me into as many parts as you wish, and I will become one again. What then of the spirit <gloss>my heart</gloss> within, which always brings forth many thousands from the tiniest part? <note>Mercury refers here to the physical property of liquid quicksilver, which easily separates into tiny spheres and just as easily merges back into a single mass.</note>

*Alch.* And

### NEW LIGHT

<meta>catchword: com-</meta>

How then must I act with you, so that you might become so?

*Merc.* I am fire within; fire is my food, but the life of fire is air, for without air, fire is extinguished. Fire prevails over air; therefore I do not rest, nor can the raw air <note>original: "crudus aer." In alchemy, "raw" or "crude" often refers to common, unrefined atmosphere, as opposed to the "philosophical air" or vital spirit.</note> constrain me. Add air to air so that both are one and hold weight; join them with hot fire, and give it to the times for safekeeping. <note>This refers to the alchemical process of "maturation" or "digestion," where a substance is kept at a steady heat for a long period to reach perfection.</note>

*Alch.* What will happen afterwards?

*Merc.* The superfluous will be drawn away, and you will burn the remainder with fire. You will place it in water and cook it; afterwards, you will give the cooked substance to the sick as medicine.

*Alch.* You tell me nothing that answers my questions. I see that you only wish to elude me with parables. Wife! Bring the pig dung! <note>Pig dung was often used in "vulgar" alchemy to provide a low, steady heat for digestion, but here it also signifies the Alchemist's descent into crude, "dirty" chemistry because he lacks spiritual understanding.</note> I will treat this *Mercury* in new ways until he tells me how the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the Elixir of Life.</note> is to be made from him.

*Mercury*, having heard these things, began to lament over the *Alchemist* and went to his mother, *Nature*. He accused the ungrateful worker. *Nature* believed her son *Mercury*, who is truthful, and in her anger...

## NEW LIGHT

How then ought one to act with you so that you might become so?

*Mercury*: I am fire within; fire is my food, but the life of fire is air. Without air, fire is extinguished. Fire prevails over air; therefore I do not rest, nor can raw air <note>original: "crudus aer." In alchemical theory, this refers to common atmospheric air which has not been "philosophically" prepared or refined.</note> constrain me. Add air to air so that both are one and hold weight; join it with hot fire, and give it to the seasons <note>original: "temporibus." This likely refers to the length of time or the specific "astrological" timing required for the alchemical digestion process.</note> to keep watch over.

*Alchemist*: What will happen after that?

*Mercury*: The superfluous will be taken away; you will burn the residue with fire, place it in water, and cook it. Afterwards, you shall give the cooked result to the sick as medicine.

*Alchemist*: You tell me nothing that answers my questions! I see that you only wish to mock me with parables. Wife, bring me pig dung! I will treat this *Mercury* in new ways until he tells me how the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>original: "Lapis Philosophicus." The supreme object of alchemy, said to possess the power to transmute metals and heal all diseases.</note> is to be made from him.

**Mercury**, having heard these things, begins to lament over the Alchemist and goes to his mother **Nature**; he accuses the ungrateful worker. **Nature** believes her son **Mercury**, who is truthful; [and moved by] anger...

*Alchemist*. Who is it that calls me? 

*Nature*. Fool! What are you doing with my son? Why do you do him such great injury? Why do you torture him? He wishes to do every good thing for you, if only you were willing to understand!

*Alchemist*. What Devil rebukes me, such a great Man and Philosopher?

This is a harsh insult, literally "dung of the philosophers," contrasting the Alchemist's perceived status with his actual ignorance.</note> full of pride! I know the true Philosophers and all the Wise Men <note>original: "Sapientes." In alchemical literature, the "Wise" are those who understand the secret operations of nature and work in harmony with her, rather than using force.</note>, whom I love, for they also love me; and they do everything according to my pleasure, and where I am unable to act, they assist me.

In this context, Nature refers to the metallic elements and chemical substances, specifically Mercury, whom the Alchemist has been mistreating with "pig dung" and crude fire.</note> well, but you accomplish nothing; and if you wished to consider the matter correctly, it is not you who treat them, but...

### NEW LIGHT

...they are the ones who treat you: for you can make nothing out of them, nor do you know how, but they, when they wish, make fools out of you. 

***Alchemist.*** It is not true; I am also a **Philosopher**, and I know how to work well. I have served more than one Prince <note>original: "apud Principem non vnum." Successful alchemists often sought patronage at royal courts; the Alchemist is using his resume to justify his skill.</note>, and among them I was considered a Philosopher—as my wife well knows. Even now I possess a **manuscript book** which was hidden in a certain wall for several hundred years. Now I shall certainly know how to make the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>original: "Lapidem Philosophorum." The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life.</note>, and it was even revealed to me just these past few days through a **dream**. Oh, I am accustomed to having true dreams! Wife, you know it is so.

***Nature.*** You will do just as your other colleagues have done, who in the beginning know everything and think they are wise, but in the end know nothing. 

***Alchemist.*** Yet others are accustomed to making it from you—if you are indeed the true **Nature**. 

***Nature.*** It is true, but only those who have known me <note>original: "qui me nouerunt." Nature emphasizes that success comes from understanding her laws, not from force or secret books.</note>, and they are very few. But those who [know] me...

<summary>In this dialogue, a defensive Alchemist tries to prove his expertise to Nature by citing his service to royalty and a "secret" ancient manuscript. Nature dismisses his vanity, noting that while true mastery is possible, it is reserved for the few who actually understand her.</summary>

The alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable so it can withstand heat without evaporating.</note> them, you <term>sublime</term> <note>original: "sublimas." The process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then back into a solid, often used for purification.</note> them; where you should <term>calcine</term> <note>original: "calcinare." Heating a liquid to create vapor and then condensing it back into a liquid.</note> them; especially this most obedient son of mine, **Mercury**, whom you torture with so many corrosive waters <note>original: "corrosivis aquis." ***Nature.*** It is well if you know how; if not, you will harm not him, but yourself and your own wallet. For it is all the same to him: he mixes with dung just as a gem might; the **gem** is always good, and the dung does not diminish it even if it is thrown into it—when it is washed, it is the same gem it was before. ***Alchemist.***

## TREATISE

<image-desc>Square woodcut decorative ornament featuring foliate scrollwork and floral motifs, positioned behind the word TREATISE.</image-desc>

### ON

# SULPHUR. <note>In alchemical philosophy, "Sulphur" does not merely refer to the chemical element brimstone. It represents one of the three primary principles (Tria Prima) of matter, specifically the active, masculine, and "combustible" soul of a substance.</note>

<term>Treatise</term> (Latin: *Tractatus*), <term>Sulphur</term> (Latin: *Sulphure*), <term>Alchemy</term> (Latin: *Alchimia*)

<summary>This title page marks the beginning of a theoretical discourse on Sulphur, which alchemists considered the active "soul" of all metals. It transitions the book from allegorical dialogue into a more technical exploration of chemical principles.</summary>
<keywords>Sulphur, alchemy, Michael Sendivogius, Tria Prima, chemical principles, soul of metals</keywords>

--- Page 136 ---
<meta>This page follows the title page of the "Treatise on Sulphur." It introduces the author's intent and his philosophical stance on why he chose to write this specific work, maintaining the humble yet secretive tone common in alchemical literature.</meta>

# PREFACE.

<image-desc>Decorative square woodcut initial "C" featuring ornamental foliate and floral vine patterns.</image-desc>

*SINCE it is not permitted for me, kind reader <note>original: "Lector benevole"</note>, to write more openly than other ancient philosophers <term>Philosophers</term> (Latin: *Philosophi*), in this context, refers specifically to alchemists and natural philosophers who studied the secrets of matter. have written; perhaps you will not be satisfied with my writings: Especially since you have so many other books by other philosophers at hand: but believe me, I have no need to write books, because I seek from them neither profit nor vain ostentation; therefore, I do not even wish to make public who I am. <note>The author, Michael Sendivogius, often published under the pseudonym "Cosmopolita" to maintain the traditional secrecy of the alchemical craft.</note> Those things which I have already made public for your benefit seemed to me more than sufficient; the rest I have intended to set aside for the Harmony <note>Likely referring to a broader work on the "Harmony of the World" or natural order that the author planned or had written.</note> where I have proposed to write extensively on natural things: nevertheless, at the urging of friends, I was obliged to write this treatise on Sulphur; in which I do not know if it is necessary to add anything to what has gone before. Indeed, this work will not satisfy you either, if the writings of so many other philosophers do not: And especially if to you, daily—*

<meta>catchword: tidia-</meta>

<summary>In this preface, the author justifies his decision to write a specific treatise on Sulphur despite his preference for anonymity and his belief that enough has already been written on the subject. # ON SULFUR
## SECOND
### *Of the Principles.*

Sulfur is not the least among the **Principles** <term>Principles: Known as the *Tria Prima* (Three Primaries), these are the fundamental spiritual and physical components—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—that alchemists believed made up all material things.</term>, because it is a part of **Metal**; indeed, it is the principal part of the **Philosophers' Stone** <term>Philosophers' Stone: The legendary substance of alchemical transformation, capable of purifying metals and granting long life.</term>: and many **Sages** <note>original: "Sapientes." The author refers to the lineage of ancient and medieval masters of the "hermetic" arts.</note> have left behind various and most true teachings concerning Sulfur. Indeed, **Geber** <note>Geber is the Latinized name for a 13th-century author (likely Paul of Taranto) writing in the name of the 8th-century chemist Jabir ibn Hayyan. He was the most authoritative figure in medieval Western alchemy.</note> himself, in the first book, chapter 28 of the *Sum of Perfection* <note>original: "Summæ Perfectionis." This was the most influential alchemical textbook in Europe for centuries.</note> says: *“By the Most High God, it [Sulfur] illuminates every body; since it is light from light, and a **tincture** <term>Tincture: In alchemical terms, a substance or "stain" that possesses the power to permeate and transform the color and nature of another material.</term>.”* 

We, however, before we begin to treat of it, have decided first to describe the origin of the principles; especially since **Sulfur** was considered the chief of the **Principles** in ancient times. Yet, where these Principles originate, few until now have shown; and it is difficult to judge any of the principles, just as...

...and of any other thing whose origin and generation is unknown: for what can a blind man say of color? Therefore, what our predecessors have omitted, we have proposed to fulfill in this **Treatise**.

Now, the **principles** of things, especially of **Metals**, according to the ancient philosophers are two: **Sulfur** and **Mercury**. But according to the **Moderns** <term>Moderns: Refers to the "Neoterics," the alchemists of the author's era—primarily the 16th and 17th centuries—who followed Paracelsus's addition of Salt to the traditional two principles.</term> they are three: **Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury**. However, the origin of these principles is the four **Elements**; and it is with their origin that we must first begin. 

Let the students of this **Sacred Science** <note>original: "S. Scientię," short for *Sacrae Scientiae*. This reflects the view of alchemy as a divinely inspired pursuit rather than just a physical craft.</note> know, therefore, that there are four Elements, and each of these four has in its center another Element from which it is elementalized. These are the four **Statues of the World** <note>original: "Statuæ Mundi." This refers to the foundational pillars or structural supports that uphold the physical universe.</note>, separated from **Chaos** in the **Creation of the World** by **Divine Wisdom**. These keep this "machine of the World" <note>original: "machinam Mundi." A common Renaissance metaphor describing the universe as a complex, divinely-designed mechanism.</note> in balance and proportion through their opposing actions, and by the inclination of celestial virtues, they produce all things below and above the earth. We shall speak of these in their proper place; here, let us return to our proposed...

## ON THE ELEMENT

->*of Earth.*<-

Earth is an Element sufficiently worthy in its quality and dignity. In this Element, the other three—and especially Fire—rest. It is a most excellent Element for concealing and manifesting those things which have been entrusted to it. It is gross <term>Gross: Meaning coarse, dense, or tangibly material, in contrast to the "subtle" or spiritual nature of the higher elements like Air or Fire.</term> and porous; it is heavy with respect to its smallness, but light with respect to its nature. It is also the Center of the World, as well as the center of the other Elements. Through its center passes the Axis of the World <note>original: "Axis Mundi." In the geocentric model of the time, the Earth was fixed at the center of the universe, with a theoretical line passing through the poles around which the heavens rotated.</note> between both Poles. It is porous, as we have said, like a sponge which brings forth nothing from itself. But whatever the other three elements drip and cast into it, it receives all; it preserves what must be preserved, and manifests what must be manifested. It brings forth nothing from itself, as we said, but it is a recep-

# ON THE ELEMENT

->*Water.*<-

Water is the heaviest Element, full of oily <term>phlegm: In early chemistry and alchemy, phlegm was not just mucus but a watery, viscous, and often tasteless substance that acted as a cooling agent or medium.</term>; and it is an Element more worthy in its quality, being volatile on the outside, but truly fixed on the inside. It is cold and moist; it is tempered by the air. It is the <term>sperm of the World: the universal material or carrier that holds the potential for life and growth.</term> in which the seed of all things is preserved: it is the guardian of the seed of any thing. However, it should be known that the seed is one thing and the sperm is another <note>The author makes a vital distinction: the "seed" (original: *semen*) is the active, vitalizing spark of life, while the "sperm" (original: *sperma*) is the physical substance or "womb-like" medium that carries it.</note>. The receptacle of the Sperm is the Earth, while the receptacle of the seed is Water. Whatever the air drips into the water, caused by fire, the water offers this to the earth. Sometimes the Sperm lacks sufficient seed, due to a deficiency of the heat that digests it; for the Sperm is always in abundance, waiting for the seed which—through the <term>imagination of fire: In this context, "imagination" refers to a formative or directive power of natural heat that shapes matter.</term>—is carried into the womb by the movement of the air. And sometimes, when the seed is lacking, the sperm enters: but it exits again

They hold the opinion that [waters] are generated from the stars, and because they know no other reason why the sea does not overflow, they claim these waters are consumed in the heart of the sea. The stars indeed cause things to happen, but they do not generate them; for nothing is generated except from its own likeness within the same species. And if it were true that certain stars generated waters, then by necessity others would generate the other Elements. This <term>machine of the World: (original: *machina Mundi*) a common Renaissance term for the structured, physical universe, viewed as a divinely designed and harmonious mechanism.</term> is held together by the four Elements in such a way that one does not exceed the other by even the smallest particle, but they contend in an equilibrium of their powers; otherwise, if one were to exceed another, total ruin would follow.

The Arctic Pole possesses a <term>magnetic power of attraction: (original: *vim magneticam attrahendi*)</term>, while the Antarctic Pole possesses a magnetic power of expulsion, a phenomenon which nature demonstrates to us in the example of the magnet. ### ON SULFUR.

<sig>L 2</sig>

...to be done, as is assumed: You have an example in that instrument by which wine is drawn out from a barrel. <note>This likely refers to a "wine thief" or a siphon, used here to illustrate how pressure or natural suction can pull liquid through a vessel—a common analogy for subterranean water movement.</note> In conclusion, therefore, you should know that <term>springs: (original: *scaturigines*) the points where water bubbles up from the earth.</term> and all outlets of water are not generated from the stars, but from the **Center of the sea**, to which they eventually return, and thus they maintain a **continuous motion**. For if this were not so, nothing at all would be generated either in the earth or upon the earth; indeed, the ruin of the world would follow. 

However, lest it be objected that in the sea all waters are salty, while the waters of springs are fresh: know the reason to be that the water is distilled through the <term>pores of the earth: (original: *poros terrae*) the network of subterranean channels believed by early naturalists to act as a natural filtration system.</term>, and passing many miles through narrow places and through sands, it is sweetened by losing its saltiness: as an example of which, cisterns were invented. There are also in some places larger and wider pores and passages through which the salty water breaks out; from these, salt mines and salt fountains are subsequently obtained, such as at <term>Halle in Germany: (original: *Hallæ in Germania*) a city famous for its ancient salt-works, where brine springs reach the surface.</term> In some places, they are also constricted by heat and the salt is left behind in the sands, while the water "sweats" into other pores, as in Poland

## ON THE THREE PRINCIPLES
### Of All Things.

Having described these four elements, let us now descend to the <term>Three Principles (original: *tria principia*; the fundamental building blocks of matter in Paracelsian alchemy, namely Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury)</term> of all things. Understand now how they are produced from the four elements in this manner:

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;After Nature received the <term>feudal privilege (original: *privilegium feudi*; a legal metaphor suggesting Nature acts as a vassal or governor under God's authority)</term> over this Monarchy of the world from the Most High Creator of all things, she began to distribute territories and provinces to each according to their rank. First, she established the four Elements as the "princes" of the world. In order to fulfill the will of the Most High (within whose will Nature herself is situated), she ordained that they should act upon one another without ceasing.

For just as these **three Principles** <note>Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt</note> were produced out of the four elements, so it is necessary through a process of reduction that these also produce two: **male and female**. Then, indeed, the two should produce **one incorruptible thing**, in which those four elements will be equally pure, refined to the highest degree, and digested; and in this way, the square shall correspond to the square. <margin><image-desc>a manicule (pointing hand) points to the start of the following sentence</image-desc></margin> And this is that **quintessence** <note>original: *quinta essentia*; literally the "fifth essence," a celestial substance distinct from the four earthly elements</note> so necessary to every craftsman, which has been separated from many opposing impurities. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And thus you have in these three principles, within any natural composition, a **body**, a **spirit**, and a **hidden soul**. If you join these three together—once they have been separated and well-purified, as we have said—they will, without doubt, by imitating nature, yield the purest fruit. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For even if the **soul** <note>original: *anima*</note> is taken from the most noble place, it cannot arrive at the goal toward which it tends except through the **spirit**, which is the place and dwelling-house of the soul. If you wish to bring the soul back to her proper place, it is necessary that she be washed of every **sin** <note>original: *peccato*; in alchemy, "sin" refers to chemical impurities or "stains" that prevent a substance from reaching perfection</note>; the place also [must] be

It identifies the work as the purification of the body, spirit, and soul to imitate the perfection of nature.</summary>
